Complex Hereditary Spastic Paraplegia

Mendelian MONDO:0015150 Pathograph 53 Show in embeddings browser

Complex (also "complicated") hereditary spastic paraplegia (HSP) is the clinical-genetic category of HSP in which the core corticospinal-tract syndrome of progressive lower-limb spasticity and weakness is accompanied by additional neurologic or systemic abnormalities — such as thin corpus callosum, cognitive impairment or intellectual disability, peripheral neuropathy, cerebellar ataxia, distal amyotrophy, epilepsy, optic atrophy, pigmentary retinopathy, or dysarthria. It is defined in contrast to "pure" (uncomplicated) HSP, in which lower-limb spasticity with subtle dorsal-column impairment and urinary urgency are the only features. Complex HSP is not a single disease but a syndromic grouping (MONDO:0015150) spanning many spastic paraplegia (SPG) genetic loci, and it is stratified by inheritance into autosomal dominant (MONDO:0015087), autosomal recessive, and X-linked complex forms. The shared substrate is the same length-dependent, distal-predominant degeneration of the longest corticospinal-tract upper motor neuron axons (maximal in the thoracic spinal cord) and dorsal-column sensory fibers seen in pure HSP; the "complex" features arise because many of the mutated SPG proteins have pleiotropic housekeeping roles (endoplasmic reticulum morphogenesis, axonal transport, mitochondrial quality control, myelination, endolysosomal and autophagic trafficking, lipid metabolism) whose loss injures additional neuronal and glial populations beyond the corticospinal tract. Correlation between the clinical pure-versus-complex split and the underlying genetic type is imperfect: several loci can present as either form. Autosomal recessive complex forms (e.g., SPG11/spatacsin, SPG15/ZFYVE26/spastizin) are classically associated with thin corpus callosum and cognitive decline; X-linked complex forms include SPG1 (L1CAM) and SPG2 (PLP1). Individual gene-defined complex forms are carried as `has_subtypes` rows bound to their own MONDO classes beneath the three inheritance-stratified rows; see `notes` for the rule that routes a given SPG form here rather than to the pure HSP entry.

Autosomal Dominant HP:0000006
Autosomal dominant complex HSP (MONDO:0015087) includes loci such as SPG10 (KIF5A) and complicated presentations of SPG3A/SPG4. Penetrance is age-dependent.
Autosomal dominant inheritance
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:23897027 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Genetic penetrance in autosomal dominant HSP is age-dependent"
Autosomal dominant HSP shows age-dependent penetrance.
Autosomal Recessive HP:0000007
Autosomal recessive complex HSP includes SPG11 (spatacsin), SPG15 (ZFYVE26/spastizin), and SPG7 (paraplegin); these require biallelic pathogenic variants and are the forms most associated with thin corpus callosum and cognitive decline.
Autosomal recessive inheritance
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:20301389 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"SPG11 is inherited in an autosomal recessive manner."
Establishes autosomal recessive inheritance for the common complex form SPG11.
X-Linked HP:0001417
X-linked complex HSP includes SPG1 (L1CAM/L1 syndrome) and SPG2 (PLP1-related disorders).

Subtypes

Autosomal Dominant Complex Hereditary Spastic Paraplegia
KIF5A hgnc:6323 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in KIF5A (hgnc:6323). hgnc:6323 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. ALDH18A1 hgnc:9722 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in ALDH18A1 (hgnc:9722). hgnc:9722 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. Autosomal dominant inheritance
Autosomal dominant forms of complex HSP (MONDO:0015087), in which dominantly transmitted spastic paraplegia is accompanied by additional neurologic features. Representative loci include SPG10 (KIF5A, with peripheral neuropathy and distal amyotrophy), SPG9 (ALDH18A1, with cataracts and other systemic features), SPG17/Silver syndrome (BSCL2, with amyotrophy of the hands), and complicated presentations of otherwise "pure" dominant loci such as SPG3A (ATL1) and SPG4 (SPAST). Penetrance is age-dependent.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:23897027 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"cause both autosomal dominant HSP (SPG17), Charcot-Marie-Tooth type 2, and distal hereditary motor neuropathy type V"
SPG17 (Silver syndrome), caused by heterozygous BSCL2/seipin variants, is a representative autosomal dominant complex HSP (spastic paraplegia with distal amyotrophy), evidencing the autosomal-dominant complex-HSP subtype.
Autosomal Recessive Complex Hereditary Spastic Paraplegia
SPG11 hgnc:11226 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in SPG11 (hgnc:11226). hgnc:11226 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. ZFYVE26 hgnc:20761 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in ZFYVE26 (hgnc:20761). hgnc:20761 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. SPG7 hgnc:11237 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in SPG7 (hgnc:11237). hgnc:11237 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. FA2H hgnc:21197 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in FA2H (hgnc:21197). hgnc:21197 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. SPART hgnc:18514 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in SPART (hgnc:18514). hgnc:18514 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. AP4B1 hgnc:572 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in AP4B1 (hgnc:572). hgnc:572 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. Autosomal recessive inheritance
Autosomal recessive forms of complex HSP, the group most strongly associated with thin corpus callosum and progressive cognitive decline. SPG11 (spatacsin) is the most common form and is characteristically associated with thin corpus callosum, intellectual disability/cognitive decline, peripheral neuropathy, and pseudobulbar involvement. SPG15 (ZFYVE26/spastizin) causes complicated autosomal-recessive spastic paraplegia including Kjellin syndrome (with pigmentary maculopathy). SPG7 (paraplegin) frequently presents with cerebellar ataxia and optic atrophy reflecting mitochondrial dysfunction. Additional characteristic autosomal recessive complex forms include SPG35 (FA2H, with leukodystrophy, seizures, and intellectual disability), SPG20/Troyer syndrome (SPART/spartin, with distal amyotrophy, dysarthria, and short stature), and the AP-4-associated HSP disorders (AP4B1/AP4M1/AP4E1/AP4S1; SPG47/50/51/52), a childhood-onset complex form with severe developmental delay and microcephaly.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:23897027 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"such as SPG11 HSP, a common autosomal recessive form of HSP frequently associated with mental retardation and thin corpus callosum"
Establishes SPG11 as a common autosomal recessive complex HSP defined by cognitive impairment and thin corpus callosum.
PMID:18394578 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"the identification of ZFYVE26, which encodes a zinc-finger protein with a FYVE domain that we named spastizin, as the cause of SPG15"
Identifies ZFYVE26/spastizin as the cause of SPG15, a complicated autosomal-recessive spastic paraplegia including Kjellin syndrome. Requoted from the abstract: the previous quote was taken from the paper's title and did not verify against the abstract text.
X-Linked Complex Hereditary Spastic Paraplegia
L1CAM hgnc:6470 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in L1CAM (hgnc:6470). hgnc:6470 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. PLP1 hgnc:9086 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in PLP1 (hgnc:9086). hgnc:9086 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. X-linked inheritance
X-linked forms of complex HSP. SPG1 is part of the L1CAM (L1) syndrome spectrum — X-linked complicated hereditary spastic paraplegia type 1, within a spectrum that also includes MASA syndrome (intellectual disability, aphasia, spastic paraplegia, adducted thumbs), X-linked hydrocephalus, and corpus callosum agenesis. SPG2 lies within the PLP1-related disorder spectrum, which ranges from Pelizaeus-Merzbacher disease to spastic paraplegia 2, reflecting a CNS myelin-formation defect.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:20301657 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"complicated hereditary spastic paraplegia type 1"
L1CAM (L1 syndrome) explicitly includes X-linked complicated hereditary spastic paraplegia type 1 (SPG1).
PMID:20301361 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"to spastic paraplegia 2 (SPG2)"
PLP1-related disorders span a phenotypic range extending to X-linked spastic paraplegia 2 (SPG2).
Troyer syndrome (SPG20, SPART) MONDO:0010156
SPART hgnc:18514 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in SPART (hgnc:18514). hgnc:18514 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. Autosomal recessive inheritance
Troyer syndrome (SPG20), caused by biallelic SPART (spartin) variants. The disease-defining report describes it as an autosomal recessive complicated hereditary spastic paraplegia occurring with high frequency in the Old Order Amish, mapped to chromosome 13q12.3, with a frameshift mutation in SPG20 (now SPART). ClinGen classifies the SPART-Troyer syndrome relationship as Definitive under autosomal recessive inheritance. The SPART record in `genetic` points at this row.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:12134148 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Troyer syndrome (TRS) is an autosomal recessive complicated hereditary spastic paraplegia (HSP) that occurs with high frequency in the Old Order Amish."
Establishes Troyer syndrome as an autosomal recessive complicated (complex) HSP, which is why it is a subtype row on this entry rather than a separate entry.

Autosomal recessive spastic paraplegia type 70 (MARS1) MONDO:0018422
MARS1 hgnc:6898 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in MARS1 (hgnc:6898). hgnc:6898 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. Autosomal recessive inheritance
SPG70, attributed to MARS1 (methionyl-tRNA synthetase 1). This is the most thinly evidenced row on the entry: a PubMed search for the SPG70 designation and for MARS1/MARS together with spastic paraplegia returned no dedicated SPG70 report, and the human evidence cited here is a childhood-onset HSP case series in which MARS was the confirmed genetic etiology in two of sixteen patients. ClinGen has not curated MARS1 against any HSP entity; its only MARS1 record is Charcot-Marie-Tooth disease (Limited, autosomal dominant). The autosomal recessive designation follows the bound MONDO class label.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35303589 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Genetically confirmed etiologies included SPAST (3 patients), MARS (2), KIF1A (2), KIF5A (1), SACS (1), SPG7 (1), REEP1 (1), PNPT1 (1), MT-ATP6 (1), and ATL1 (1)."
MARS (now MARS1) was the confirmed genetic etiology in two patients of a childhood-onset hereditary spastic paraplegia cohort, which is the human evidence available for this gene-disease pair.
Autosomal recessive spastic paraplegia type 76 (CAPN1) MONDO:0014827
CAPN1 hgnc:1476 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in CAPN1 (hgnc:1476). hgnc:1476 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. Autosomal recessive inheritance
SPG76, caused by biallelic CAPN1 (calpain 1) variants. Rare homozygous and compound heterozygous nonsense, missense, frameshift and splice-site CAPN1 variants were identified in nine affected individuals from three autosomal recessive HSP families and segregated with disease. Calpain 1 is a CNS protease implicated in synaptic plasticity and in axon maturation and maintenance, which is why CAPN1 is also bound on the corticospinal-tract axonal degeneration node.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:27153400 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"mutations in CAPN1 were identified in all affected individuals, and sequencing in additional family members confirmed the segregation of these mutations with the disease (spastic paraplegia 76 [SPG76])"
Identifies biallelic CAPN1 variants as the cause of SPG76 in three autosomal recessive HSP families, with segregation confirmed.
Autosomal recessive spastic paraplegia type 78 (ATP13A2) MONDO:0014975
ATP13A2 hgnc:30213 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in ATP13A2 (hgnc:30213). hgnc:30213 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. Autosomal recessive inheritance
SPG78, caused by biallelic ATP13A2 variants. ATP13A2 is allelic with Kufor-Rakeb syndrome, which dismech curates as its own entry (Kufor-Rakeb_syndrome, MONDO:0011706); the SPG78 presentation is recorded here because the defining report frames it as complicated hereditary spastic paraplegia added to the clinical continuum of ATP13A2-associated disorders, dominated by adult-onset lower-limb predominant spastic paraparesis. ClinGen's only ATP13A2 record is Kufor-Rakeb syndrome (Definitive), not SPG78.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:28137957 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"In a Bulgarian family with three siblings affected by complicated hereditary spastic paraplegia, we performed whole exome sequencing and homozygosity mapping and identified a homozygous p.Thr512Ile (c.1535C > T) mutation in ATP13A2."
Identifies a homozygous ATP13A2 variant in a family with complicated HSP, the observation on which the SPG78 designation rests.
PMID:28137957 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Human Clinical
"Molecular defects in this gene have been causally associated with Kufor-Rakeb syndrome"
Records the allelic relationship that makes the lump/split ruling necessary: the same gene underlies Kufor-Rakeb syndrome, which is a separate dismech entry.
Glutamate pyruvate transaminase 2 deficiency MONDO:0014567
GPT2 hgnc:18062 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in GPT2 (hgnc:18062). hgnc:18062 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. Autosomal recessive inheritance
GPT2 deficiency, caused by biallelic loss-of-function GPT2 variants. Affected individuals in the defining kindreds were ascertained for intellectual and developmental disability, showed postnatal microcephaly, and most of those followed over time developed a progressive spastic paraplegia, which is why the disease is recorded on this entry. ClinGen classifies the GPT2-GPT2 deficiency relationship as Definitive under autosomal recessive inheritance against the same MONDO class bound here.

Hereditary sensory and autonomic neuropathy with spastic paraplegia (CCT5) MONDO:0009748
CCT5 hgnc:1618 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in CCT5 (hgnc:1618). hgnc:1618 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. Autosomal recessive inheritance
Mutilating sensory neuropathy with spastic paraplegia, caused by a CCT5 missense variant in a consanguineous Moroccan family with four affected members. CCT5 encodes the epsilon subunit of the cytosolic chaperonin-containing t-complex peptide-1. ClinGen has no CCT5 gene-disease validity record.

Hereditary spastic paraplegia 15 (ZFYVE26, Kjellin syndrome) MONDO:0010044
ZFYVE26 hgnc:20761 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in ZFYVE26 (hgnc:20761). hgnc:20761 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. Autosomal recessive inheritance
SPG15, caused by biallelic truncating ZFYVE26 (spastizin) variants; six different truncating mutations segregated with disease in eight families whose phenotype included variable features of Kjellin syndrome. Spastizin colocalizes partially with endoplasmic reticulum and endosomal markers, which is why ZFYVE26 is also bound on the endolysosomal and ESCRT membrane-traffic node. The ZFYVE26 record in `genetic` points at this row. ClinGen has no ZFYVE26 gene-disease validity record.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:18394578 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Six different truncating mutations were found to segregate with the disease in eight families with a phenotype that included variable clinical features of Kjellin syndrome."
Establishes biallelic truncating ZFYVE26 variants as the cause of SPG15 across eight families, including the Kjellin-syndrome presentation.
Hereditary spastic paraplegia 18 (ERLIN2) MONDO:0012639
ERLIN2 hgnc:1356 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in ERLIN2 (hgnc:1356). hgnc:1356 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. Autosomal recessive inheritance
SPG18, caused by ERLIN2 variants. ClinGen holds two ERLIN2 records against this same MONDO class: autosomal recessive is Definitive and autosomal dominant is Limited, so the recessive form is the one this row treats as established. ERLIN2 acts in inositol 1,4,5-trisphosphate receptor degradation, which is why it is bound on the endoplasmic reticulum calcium-release node alongside RNF170.

Hereditary spastic paraplegia 26 (B4GALNT1) MONDO:0012213
B4GALNT1 hgnc:4117 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in B4GALNT1 (hgnc:4117). hgnc:4117 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. Autosomal recessive inheritance
SPG26, caused by biallelic B4GALNT1 variants. Functional studies in patient-derived fibroblasts and in cell models of GM2 synthase deficiency confirmed loss of B4GALNT1 function with no synthesis of GM2 or downstream gangliosides, which is why B4GALNT1 is bound on the membrane-lipid and ganglioside node. Hereditary spastic paraplegia 43 (C19orf12) MONDO:0014024
C19orf12 hgnc:25443 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in C19orf12 (hgnc:25443). hgnc:25443 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. Autosomal recessive inheritance
SPG43, caused by a homozygous C19orf12 missense variant first reported in two Malian sisters. C19orf12 is allelic with neurodegeneration with brain iron accumulation, which dismech curates in Neurodegeneration_With_Brain_Iron_Accumulation; the two presentations are distinguished in the defining report by brain imaging, which showed no iron deposition in the SPG43 subjects. ClinGen's only C19orf12 records are for neurodegeneration with brain iron accumulation 4.

Hereditary spastic paraplegia 53 (VPS37A) MONDO:0013962
VPS37A hgnc:24928 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in VPS37A (hgnc:24928). hgnc:24928 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. Autosomal recessive inheritance
SPG53, caused by a homozygous VPS37A missense founder mutation in two kindreds of Arab Moslem origin presenting with early-onset spastic paraparesis of upper and lower limbs, mild intellectual disability, kyphosis, pectus carinatum and hypertrichosis. VPS37A is a component of the ESCRT system, which is why it is bound on the endolysosomal and ESCRT membrane-traffic node. ClinGen classifies VPS37A-complex hereditary spastic paraplegia as Limited, so the gene-disease relationship is not yet established at ClinGen even though the founder mutation segregated fully in both families.

Pathophysiology

9
Length-Dependent Corticospinal-Tract Axonal Degeneration
The core lesion shared with pure HSP: distal-predominant degeneration of the longest corticospinal-tract upper motor neuron axons (maximal in the thoracic spinal cord) together with degeneration of the dorsal-column fasciculus gracilis sensory fibers. This length-dependent, dying-back axonopathy of the longest CNS motor-sensory tracts produces the progressive lower-limb spasticity, weakness, hyperreflexia, and extensor plantar responses common to all HSP.

CL:0008048 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.

PMID:27153400 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Other
"CAPN1 encodes calpain 1, a protease that is widely present in the CNS. Calpain 1 is involved in synaptic plasticity, synaptic restructuring, and axon maturation and maintenance."
Assigns calpain 1 a role in axon maturation and maintenance, the process whose failure this node describes, which is why CAPN1 is bound here.
Pleiotropic SPG-Protein Dysfunction Beyond the Corticospinal Tract
In complex HSP the mutated SPG proteins have diverse housekeeping functions — axonal transport, endoplasmic reticulum tubular network morphogenesis, mitochondrial quality control, myelin formation, and endolysosomal/autophagic membrane trafficking. Loss of these functions injures neuronal and glial populations beyond the corticospinal tract (cortical neurons, cerebellar neurons, peripheral nerve, retina, and oligodendrocyte myelin), producing the additional "complicating" features (thin corpus callosum, cognitive decline, ataxia, neuropathy, retinopathy) that distinguish complex from pure HSP. This pleiotropy explains why complex forms cluster among loci with broad subcellular roles (e.g., SPG11/SPG15 endolysosomal-autophagy, SPG7 mitochondrial, SPG2/PLP1 myelin).

Endolysosomal and ESCRT Membrane-Traffic Failure
A mechanism class shared by complex-HSP genes whose products act in endosomal sorting, endosome maturation and lysosomal transport. Spastizin (ZFYVE26) localizes partly to endoplasmic reticulum and endosomal compartments; spartin (SPART) resembles molecules of endosomal trafficking; VPS37A is a subunit of the ESCRT system that matures multivesicular bodies; and ATP13A2 is a lysosomal transport ATPase. The AP-4 adaptor complex is curated as its own node because its characterised defect is autophagosome formation rather than endosomal transport. Every gene bound here causes a complex HSP recorded on this entry, and the node's downstream edge records that failure feeding the pleiotropic SPG-protein dysfunction the entry models.

AP-4-Dependent ATG9A Export and Autophagosome Formation Failure
The AP-4 adaptor complex associates with the trans-Golgi network and exports ATG9A from it; in AP-4-deficient cells that export fails, reducing ATG9A delivery to pre-autophagosomal structures and impairing autophagosome formation. All four AP-4 subunit genes are curated on this node because biallelic variants in any of them cause the same disease, AP-4-associated HSP, which this entry carries as a single subtype row. The node is kept separate from the endolysosomal membrane-traffic node because the characterised AP-4 defect is autophagosome biogenesis rather than endosomal transport.

Neuronal Membrane-Lipid and Ganglioside Metabolic Failure
A second recurrent mechanism class among complex-HSP genes, whose products are lipid-modifying enzymes. DDHD2 is an intracellular phospholipase A1 and its loss produces an abnormal cerebral lipid peak detectable by magnetic resonance spectroscopy; PNPLA6 encodes neuropathy target esterase, which maintains phospholipid homeostasis in the nervous system; ABHD16A is the main brain phosphatidylserine hydrolase; and B4GALNT1 loss abolishes synthesis of GM2 and the downstream complex gangliosides. Each of these genes causes a complex HSP recorded on this entry, and the DDHD2 report shows the lipid disturbance directly in patients by cerebral magnetic resonance spectroscopy.

+ 2 more references
Mitochondrial Iron-Sulfur Cluster Assembly Failure
IBA57 is the mitochondrial iron-sulfur protein assembly factor. The SPG74 splice-site variant leaves ten-fold lower levels of wild-type IBA57 protein and reduces the levels and activities of several mitochondrial 4Fe-4S proteins including respiratory complexes I and II, while 2Fe-2S proteins remain normal. This node is one of two mitochondrial mechanisms on the entry - the other, impaired anaplerosis, is a separate node because GPT2 takes no part in iron-sulfur cluster assembly - and both are counterparts of the SPG7/paraplegin mitochondrial mechanism already recorded here.

Impaired Mitochondrial Anaplerosis
GPT2 is a mitochondrial transaminase. Its biallelic variants are biochemically loss-of-function, and loss of Gpt2 produces defects in anaplerosis, the process that replenishes tricarboxylic-acid-cycle intermediates. This is a separate node from the iron-sulfur cluster assembly mechanism because IBA57 takes no part in transamination or anaplerosis, and GPT2 takes no part in iron-sulfur cluster assembly.

Kinesin-Dependent Axonal Cargo Transport Failure
Kinesins are cargo/motor proteins whose mutation is an established cause of hereditary spastic paraplegia. Two complex forms on this entry act through this machinery: spastic ataxia 2 is caused by homozygous KIF1C variants, and SPOAN syndrome by a non-coding deletion upstream of KLC2 that raises KLC2 expression in patient fibroblasts and patient-derived motor neurons - a gain, not a loss, of gene product. Both diseases are recorded as subtype rows on this entry, so this node's downstream consequence is the length-dependent corticospinal-tract axonopathy of the entry's core node.

IP3-Receptor-Dependent Endoplasmic Reticulum Calcium Release Dysregulation
Calcium efflux from the endoplasmic reticulum into the cytoplasm is controlled by inositol 1,4,5-trisphosphate binding to its receptor, and activated receptors are then degraded by the endoplasmic-reticulum-associated degradation pathway. Two complex-HSP genes on this entry act in that degradation step: ERLIN2, whose biallelic variants cause SPG18, and RNF170, the ubiquitin E3 ligase that targets the receptors and whose bi-allelic variants cause SPG85. The RNF170 report names inositol 1,4,5-trisphosphate signalling as a candidate key pathway for the hereditary spastic paraplegias, which is the claim this node records.

click to show 
name: Complex Hereditary Spastic Paraplegia
creation_date: "2026-07-01T00:00:00Z"
category: Mendelian
description: >
  Complex (also "complicated") hereditary spastic paraplegia (HSP) is the
  clinical-genetic category of HSP in which the core corticospinal-tract
  syndrome of progressive lower-limb spasticity and weakness is accompanied by
  additional neurologic or systemic abnormalities — such as thin corpus
  callosum, cognitive impairment or intellectual disability, peripheral
  neuropathy, cerebellar ataxia, distal amyotrophy, epilepsy, optic atrophy,
  pigmentary retinopathy, or dysarthria. It is defined in contrast to "pure"
  (uncomplicated) HSP, in which lower-limb spasticity with subtle dorsal-column
  impairment and urinary urgency are the only features. Complex HSP is not a
  single disease but a syndromic grouping (MONDO:0015150) spanning many spastic
  paraplegia (SPG) genetic loci, and it is stratified by inheritance into
  autosomal dominant (MONDO:0015087), autosomal recessive, and X-linked complex
  forms. The shared substrate is the same length-dependent, distal-predominant
  degeneration of the longest corticospinal-tract upper motor neuron axons
  (maximal in the thoracic spinal cord) and dorsal-column sensory fibers seen in
  pure HSP; the "complex" features arise because many of the mutated SPG
  proteins have pleiotropic housekeeping roles (endoplasmic reticulum
  morphogenesis, axonal transport, mitochondrial quality control, myelination,
  endolysosomal and autophagic trafficking, lipid metabolism) whose loss injures
  additional neuronal and glial populations beyond the corticospinal tract.
  Correlation between the clinical pure-versus-complex split and the underlying
  genetic type is imperfect: several loci can present as either form. Autosomal
  recessive complex forms (e.g., SPG11/spatacsin, SPG15/ZFYVE26/spastizin) are
  classically associated with thin corpus callosum and cognitive decline;
  X-linked complex forms include SPG1 (L1CAM) and SPG2 (PLP1). Individual
  gene-defined complex forms are carried as `has_subtypes` rows bound to their own
  MONDO classes beneath the three inheritance-stratified rows; see `notes` for the
  rule that routes a given SPG form here rather than to the pure HSP entry.

- name: AD Complex HSP
  display_name: Autosomal Dominant Complex Hereditary Spastic Paraplegia
  description: >
    Autosomal dominant forms of complex HSP (MONDO:0015087), in which
    dominantly transmitted spastic paraplegia is accompanied by additional
    neurologic features. Representative loci include SPG10 (KIF5A, with
    peripheral neuropathy and distal amyotrophy), SPG9 (ALDH18A1, with
    cataracts and other systemic features), SPG17/Silver syndrome (BSCL2, with
    amyotrophy of the hands), and complicated presentations of otherwise
    "pure" dominant loci such as SPG3A (ATL1) and SPG4 (SPAST). Penetrance is
    age-dependent.

- name: AR Complex HSP
  display_name: Autosomal Recessive Complex Hereditary Spastic Paraplegia
  description: >
    Autosomal recessive forms of complex HSP, the group most strongly
    associated with thin corpus callosum and progressive cognitive decline.
    SPG11 (spatacsin) is the most common form and is characteristically
    associated with thin corpus callosum, intellectual disability/cognitive
    decline, peripheral neuropathy, and pseudobulbar involvement. SPG15
    (ZFYVE26/spastizin) causes complicated autosomal-recessive spastic
    paraplegia including Kjellin syndrome (with pigmentary maculopathy). SPG7
    (paraplegin) frequently presents with cerebellar ataxia and optic atrophy
    reflecting mitochondrial dysfunction. Additional characteristic autosomal
    recessive complex forms include SPG35 (FA2H, with leukodystrophy, seizures,
    and intellectual disability), SPG20/Troyer syndrome (SPART/spartin, with
    distal amyotrophy, dysarthria, and short stature), and the AP-4-associated
    HSP disorders (AP4B1/AP4M1/AP4E1/AP4S1; SPG47/50/51/52), a childhood-onset
    complex form with severe developmental delay and microcephaly.

    snippet: >-

classifications:

pathophysiology:
- name: Length-Dependent Corticospinal-Tract Axonal Degeneration
  conforms_to: "corticospinal_tract_axonopathy#Distal Length-Dependent Degeneration of Long CNS Axons"
  description: >
    The core lesion shared with pure HSP: distal-predominant degeneration of the
    longest corticospinal-tract upper motor neuron axons (maximal in the
    thoracic spinal cord) together with degeneration of the dorsal-column
    fasciculus gracilis sensory fibers. This length-dependent, dying-back
    axonopathy of the longest CNS motor-sensory tracts produces the progressive
    lower-limb spasticity, weakness, hyperreflexia, and extensor plantar
    responses common to all HSP.
  genes:
  - preferred_term: CAPN1
    term:
      id: hgnc:1476
      label: CAPN1
    description: >-
      Bound here because the cited report assigns calpain 1 a role in axon maturation and
      maintenance, which is the process this node describes; SPG76 is its disease row.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:27153400
    reference_title: 'Mutations in CAPN1 Cause Autosomal-Recessive Hereditary Spastic Paraplegia.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    quote_role: BACKGROUND
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      CAPN1 encodes calpain 1, a protease that is widely present in the CNS. Calpain 1 is involved
      in synaptic plasticity, synaptic restructuring, and axon maturation and maintenance.
    explanation: >-
      Assigns calpain 1 a role in axon maturation and maintenance, the process whose failure this
      node describes, which is why CAPN1 is bound here.
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: Corticospinal-tract upper motor neuron
    term:
      id: CL:0008048
      label: upper motor neuron
  biological_processes:
  - preferred_term: Axonal transport
    term:
      id: GO:0098930
      label: axonal transport
    modifier: DECREASED
  downstream:
  - target: Lower-Limb Spastic Paraplegia
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:23897027
      reference_title: "Hereditary spastic paraplegia: clinico-pathologic features and emerging molecular mechanisms."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "in which bilateral lower extremity weakness and spasticity (each of variable degree) are the predominant"
      explanation: >
        The length-dependent corticospinal-tract axonal degeneration produces
        the predominant lower-limb spasticity and weakness of HSP.
  - target: Lower-Limb Weakness
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:23897027
      reference_title: "Hereditary spastic paraplegia: clinico-pathologic features and emerging molecular mechanisms."
      supports: SUPPORT

      snippet: "degeneration of corticospinal tract \naxons (maximal in the thoracic spinal cord) and degeneration of fasciculus \ngracilis fibers"
      explanation: >
        Postmortem studies consistently show the length-dependent corticospinal
        and dorsal-column axonal degeneration underlying the HSP syndrome.
    - reference: PMID:23897027
      reference_title: "Hereditary spastic paraplegia: clinico-pathologic features and emerging molecular mechanisms."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "HSP syndromes thus \nappear to involve motor-sensory axon degeneration affecting predominantly (but \nnot exclusively) the distal ends of long central nervous system (CNS) axons"
      explanation: >
        Frames HSP as a distal, length-dependent motor-sensory axonopathy of the
        longest CNS axons.
- name: Pleiotropic SPG-Protein Dysfunction Beyond the Corticospinal Tract
  description: >
    In complex HSP the mutated SPG proteins have diverse housekeeping functions
    — axonal transport, endoplasmic reticulum tubular network morphogenesis,
    mitochondrial quality control, myelin formation, and endolysosomal/autophagic
    membrane trafficking. Loss of these functions injures neuronal and glial
    populations beyond the corticospinal tract (cortical neurons, cerebellar
    neurons, peripheral nerve, retina, and oligodendrocyte myelin), producing
    the additional "complicating" features (thin corpus callosum, cognitive
    decline, ataxia, neuropathy, retinopathy) that distinguish complex from pure
    HSP. This pleiotropy explains why complex forms cluster among loci with
    broad subcellular roles (e.g., SPG11/SPG15 endolysosomal-autophagy,
    SPG7 mitochondrial, SPG2/PLP1 myelin).
  genes:
  - preferred_term: SPG11

- name: Endolysosomal and ESCRT Membrane-Traffic Failure
  description: >-
    A mechanism class shared by complex-HSP genes whose products act in endosomal sorting,
    endosome maturation and lysosomal transport. Spastizin (ZFYVE26) localizes partly to
    endoplasmic reticulum and endosomal compartments; spartin (SPART) resembles molecules of
    endosomal trafficking; VPS37A is a subunit of the ESCRT system that matures multivesicular
    bodies; and ATP13A2 is a lysosomal transport ATPase. The AP-4 adaptor complex is curated as
    its own node because its characterised defect is autophagosome formation rather than
    endosomal transport. Every gene bound here causes a complex HSP recorded on this entry, and
    the node's downstream edge records that failure feeding the pleiotropic SPG-protein
    dysfunction the entry models.

- name: AP-4-Dependent ATG9A Export and Autophagosome Formation Failure
  description: >-

    this node because biallelic variants in any of them cause the same disease, AP-4-associated
    HSP, which this entry carries as a single subtype row. The node is kept separate from the
    endolysosomal membrane-traffic node because the characterised AP-4 defect is autophagosome
    biogenesis rather than endosomal transport.

- name: Neuronal Membrane-Lipid and Ganglioside Metabolic Failure
  description: >-
    A second recurrent mechanism class among complex-HSP genes, whose products are lipid-modifying
    enzymes. DDHD2 is an intracellular phospholipase A1 and its loss produces an abnormal cerebral
    lipid peak detectable by magnetic resonance spectroscopy; PNPLA6 encodes neuropathy target
    esterase, which maintains phospholipid homeostasis in the nervous system; ABHD16A is the main
    brain phosphatidylserine hydrolase; and B4GALNT1 loss abolishes synthesis of GM2 and the
    downstream complex gangliosides. Each of these genes causes a complex HSP
    recorded on this entry, and the DDHD2 report shows the lipid disturbance directly in patients
    by cerebral magnetic resonance spectroscopy.

- name: Kinesin-Dependent Axonal Cargo Transport Failure
  description: >-
    Kinesins are cargo/motor proteins whose mutation is an established cause of hereditary spastic
    paraplegia. Two complex forms on this entry act through this machinery: spastic ataxia 2 is
    caused by homozygous KIF1C variants, and SPOAN syndrome by a non-coding deletion upstream of
    KLC2 that raises KLC2 expression in patient fibroblasts and patient-derived motor neurons - a
    gain, not a loss, of gene product. Both diseases are recorded as subtype rows on this
    entry, so this node's downstream consequence is the length-dependent corticospinal-tract
    axonopathy of the entry's core node.

- name: IP3-Receptor-Dependent Endoplasmic Reticulum Calcium Release Dysregulation
  description: >-
    Calcium efflux from the endoplasmic reticulum into the cytoplasm is controlled by inositol
    1,4,5-trisphosphate binding to its receptor, and activated receptors are then degraded by the
    endoplasmic-reticulum-associated degradation pathway. Two complex-HSP genes on this entry act
    in that degradation step: ERLIN2, whose biallelic variants cause SPG18, and RNF170, the
    ubiquitin E3 ligase that targets the receptors and whose bi-allelic variants cause SPG85. The RNF170
    report names inositol 1,4,5-trisphosphate signalling as a candidate key pathway for the
    hereditary spastic paraplegias, which is the claim this node records.

- name: Autosomal Recessive
  inheritance_term:
    preferred_term: Autosomal recessive inheritance
    term:
      id: HP:0000007
      label: Autosomal recessive inheritance
  description: >
    Autosomal recessive complex HSP includes SPG11 (spatacsin), SPG15
    (ZFYVE26/spastizin), and SPG7 (paraplegin); these require biallelic
    pathogenic variants and are the forms most associated with thin corpus
    callosum and cognitive decline.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:20301389
    reference_title: "Spastic Paraplegia 11."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "SPG11 is inherited in an autosomal recessive manner."
    explanation: Establishes autosomal recessive inheritance for the common complex form SPG11.

    hereditary spastic paraplegia) and for MARS1 or MARS together with spastic paraplegia returned
    no dedicated SPG70 report, so the human evidence cited here is a childhood-onset HSP case
    series in which MARS was the confirmed genetic etiology in two of sixteen patients. The
    committed ClinGen gene-validity snapshot holds one MARS1 record, Charcot-Marie-Tooth disease
    (Limited, autosomal dominant), and none against an HSP entity. The autosomal recessive mode
    recorded here follows the bound MONDO class label rather than a cited study.

notes: >
  Complex HSP (MONDO:0015150) is a syndromic grouping term rather than a single
  gene disease; the autosomal dominant complex form is MONDO:0015087. It is the
  sibling of pure/uncomplicated HSP under the broader hereditary spastic
  paraplegia entry (MONDO:0019064), which carries the shared corticospinal-tract
  pathophysiology and the gene-level SPG subtypes. The pure-versus-complex clinical
  split correlates imperfectly with genetic type, and many loci can present as
  either form.

  Subtype structure. The entry carries two layers of `has_subtypes` rows. Three
  rows (AD, AR, XL Complex HSP) stratify the category by inheritance and list
  representative loci; some genes named there have their own disease entries
  instead of a row here (ALDH18A1, for example, is curated as ALDH18A1_Cutis_Laxa
  and ALDH18A1_De_Barsy_Spectrum). Beneath them sit gene-defined rows, each bound
  to its own MONDO class and carrying the causal gene, so that a specific complex
  form is addressable without a separate disease file. A gene's `genetic` record
  points at the gene-defined row wherever one exists, and at an inheritance row
  only where it does not; the gene-defined target is the more informative of the
  two.
  Routing between this entry and the pure HSP entry follows the rule recorded
  there: a gene-defined SPG form sits on the pure entry when the literature puts
  it in the pure clinical category and here when it puts it in the complex
  category. Every gene-defined row added here is placed in the complex category
  by the source cited on that row or by the label of the MONDO class it binds.

  Anchor. The `disease_term` binding MONDO:0015150 was checked against the
  over-broad-anchor pattern of dismech#12184 and left in place: this entry is the
  syndromic grouping, not a gene-specific disease wearing a grouping term, and
  MONDO defines the class as "A hereditary spastic paraplegia that is part of a
  larger syndrome" (read from OLS 2026-09-26). ClinGen independently curates five
  of the genes recorded here - DSTYK, B4GALNT1, VPS37A, ENTPD1 and MAG - directly
  against MONDO:0015150, so the anchor is the class the field itself uses for
  these gene-disease relationships.

  AP-4-associated HSP is one row with four genes, not four rows. GeneReviews has
  a single AP-4-HSP chapter whose diagnostic criterion is biallelic variants in
  AP4B1, AP4E1, AP4M1 or AP4S1; ClinGen curates all four subunit genes as
  Definitive against one entity, AP-4 deficiency syndrome (MONDO:0100176); and
  MONDO supplies that integrated class itself, defined as "A genetic disorder
  associated with variation(s) in the AP4 genes: AP4B1, AP4E1, AP4M1, and AP4S1".
  MONDO also keeps four gene-generated classes for the same biology
  (MONDO:0013551 SPG47/AP4B1, MONDO:0013048 SPG50/AP4M1, MONDO:0013401
  SPG51/AP4E1, MONDO:0013552 SPG52/AP4S1), each defined in the bare form "Any
  hereditary spastic paraplegia in which the cause of the disease is a mutation in
  the <gene> gene". Carrying both granularities is not an ontology defect: the
  same bare definition form appears on MONDO:0014975 (SPG78/ATP13A2) and
  MONDO:0014729 (SPG75/MAG), two classes this entry does bind, so an argument
  against it would condemn rows kept here as well as rows declined.

  Allelic-series routings, recorded so the same gene is not curated twice. SPG78
  (ATP13A2) is allelic with Kufor-Rakeb syndrome, which has its own entry;
  SPG43 (C19orf12) with neurodegeneration with brain iron accumulation, which has
  its own entry; SPG39 (PNPLA6) with the Boucher-Neuhauser presentation, which has
  its own entry; SPG85 (RNF170) with autosomal dominant sensory ataxia 1, which
  has its own entry and already lists SPG85 as a differential diagnosis; SPG63
  (AMPD2) with pontocerebellar hypoplasia type 9; and SPG74 (IBA57) with the
  mitochondrial-disease entity ClinGen curates. In each case the
  spastic-paraplegia presentation is what this entry holds.

  Sources consulted. GeneReviews (the AP-4-HSP, SPG11, L1 syndrome, PLP1 and pure
  HSP overview chapters), the committed ClinGen gene-disease validity snapshot,
  and primary literature. Orphanet could not be consulted: no ORPHA_*.md record
  for any of these spastic-paraplegia concepts is present in references_cache, and
  populating one needs a maintainer-level Orphadata refresh.

  As more specific complex-HSP leaf entries are curated, a kb/groupings/ union
  over them would be a natural follow-up.
